# sehatly-telemedicine-platform - Work Plan

## TL;DR (For humans)

<!-- Fill this LAST, after the detailed plan below is written, so it summarizes the REAL plan. -->
<!-- Plain English for a non-engineer: NO file paths, NO todo numbers, NO wave/agent/tool names. -->

**What you'll get:** A working telemedicine platform with one backend API and a browser client for patients, doctors, pharmacists and admins — plus a complete, generated integration contract so a separate Flutter team can build the phone app without reading your PHP. Patients register with a phone number and one-time code, browse and book doctors, chat and video-consult, receive electronic prescriptions and medical records, order medicine with payment and delivery tracking, and get in-app notifications. Doctors get a schedule/slot calendar, a consultation workspace with a SOAP form, e-prescribing with automatic drug-interaction and allergy warnings, and pharmacist verification of prescriptions. Pharmacists verify prescriptions; audit trails record every access to a medical record, as Indonesian medical-privacy law requires.

**Why this approach:** The three biggest decisions are that the existing starter app is converted into a pure JSON API rather than a server-rendered site, that the database schema in your SQL file is treated as untouchable law and reproduced table-by-table with an automated checker that diffs the live database against the file, and that the mobile app is served by a **generated contract** — an OpenAPI spec plus a ready-made Dart client package — instead of prose documentation, so the Flutter team's models and method signatures cannot drift from the backend.

**What it will NOT do:** It will not build the Flutter app itself — it delivers the contract, a reusable Dart client package, and a written integration guide instead, and it will not touch your `mobile/` folder if you have one. It will not edit `telemedicine_test.sql`. It will not build endpoints for the 14 tables no module assigned (reviews, articles, home care, laboratory, BPJS claims, insurance cards) — those get database tables and models only, and are listed as a known gap. It will not connect to a real SMS provider, payment gateway, video SDK, SATUSEHAT, or BPJS V-Claim; those are stubbed behind interfaces with working data shapes. It will not delete or overwrite your uncommitted work.

**Effort:** XL
**Risk:** High - the backend is a live app with uncommitted changes that must be converted in place, and 75 database tables must match your SQL file exactly.
**Decisions I made for you:** monorepo with the Laravel app at the root and a new `web/` folder, reusing your existing 33 UI components; MySQL 8 for both development and tests plus an automated schema-diff checker; deterministic encryption for the national ID number so its unique index still works; a generated OpenAPI contract plus a pure-Dart client package as the mobile handoff instead of hand-written docs; the `reverb` broadcast driver rather than the older `pusher` workaround; TypeScript 7; and a required CSS setting so your existing frosted-glass UI components keep rendering.

Your next move: approve this plan, or ask me to run a high-accuracy review of it first. Full execution detail follows below.

---

> TL;DR (machine): XL / High. Converting an in-fused Laravel 13 + Inertia starter into a pure `/api/v1` Sanctum API on MySQL 8; 75 migrations mirroring `telemedicine_test.sql` 1:1 plus 2 views and an information_schema parity verifier; a new React 19 SPA in `web/`; a **generated** mobile handoff (OpenAPI 3.1 spec + pure-Dart client package + integration guide + enum catalogues + contract-conformance suite) in place of a Flutter app; 5 module waves (Auth, Booking, Konsultasi, Resep, Pesanan/Audit) each shipping backend + web + tests; cross-cutting compliance wave (audit, PDP consent, NIK encryption, timezone, throttling).

## Scope

### Must have

- **Repo topology (Q1=A, revised).** Repo root = the Laravel 13 API. New `web/` = React 19 SPA. Inertia is removed from the API surface. The 26 existing shadcn/Radix components in `resources/js/components/ui/` are **relocated** into `web/src/components/ui/`, not rewritten. **No Flutter app is built.** The mobile handoff is delivered instead as `packages/sehatly_api_client/` (a pure-Dart, Flutter-free package) plus generated artefacts under `docs/`.
- **Database engine (Q2=A).** MySQL 8 for dev *and* test. `telemedisin_db` for dev, `telemedisin_db_test` for tests. `phpunit.xml:26-27` is rewritten off in-memory SQLite. A parity verifier diffs live `information_schema` against `telemedicine_test.sql`.
- **Schema fidelity.** All **75** `CREATE TABLE` statements reproduced as 75 Laravel migrations in the SQL's own dependency-safe order, plus the section `[14]` deferred FK, plus both views. Seeders port SQL section `[16]`.
- **NIK encryption (Q3=A).** Deterministic encryption for `pasien.nik` (fixed IV derived via HMAC from `APP_KEY`) so the existing `UNIQUE` index keeps working. No edit to the SQL file.
- **Token storage guidance (Q4=A, now advisory).** Because no Flutter app is built here, Q4 becomes a **documented recommendation for the mobile team**: `flutter_secure_storage` 11.2.0 for access + refresh tokens, `GetStorage` for theme/locale/profile/FCM token. The Dart package delivered in task 24 implements exactly this split, with the OWASP rationale in its README, so the mobile team inherits the secure default rather than re-deciding it.
- **API kernel.** `/api/v1` prefix via `apiPrefix`, Sanctum bearer tokens, uniform `{success,data,message}` / `{success,message,errors}` envelope, API Resources, ISO-8601 UTC, `meta` pagination, CORS, `permission:` and `tipe:` middleware.
- **Modules 1-5**, each with migrations, models+relations+casts, FormRequests, API Resources, controllers, services, Pest feature tests (happy + >=1 failure), and React screens — per the spec's per-module summary requirement.
- **Mobile handoff (replaces the Flutter deliverable).** Five concrete artefacts, each a todo below:
  1. `packages/sehatly_api_client/` — a pure-Dart package (no Flutter dependency) with a `Dio` client, a single-flight refresh interceptor, the secure/plain storage split, the error and pagination models, and DTOs generated from the OpenAPI spec. Fully unit-tested and usable as-is.
  2. `docs/openapi.yaml` — OpenAPI 3.1, generated from the live route table and every `FormRequest`, so it cannot drift.
  3. `docs/mobile-integration.md` — the human guide: auth lifecycle, error catalogue, pagination, timezone, uploads, NIK masking, realtime chat, push notifications, a worked end-to-end example, and a troubleshooting table.
  4. `docs/enums.json` + `/api/v1/referensi/*` endpoints — every ENUM value list in the 75-table schema, machine-readable, so the mobile team can build every dropdown and badge without guessing a string.
  5. A **contract-conformance suite** — Pest tests that assert real responses validate against the OpenAPI schemas, so the mobile team's generated models are provably correct.
- **Realtime chat** over Reverb with a private channel per consultation, consumed by the web client via `laravel-echo` and documented for mobile via the Pusher protocol.
- **Compliance.** `audit_log` written by global Eloquent observers (never by hand in controllers), `akses_rekam_medis_log` on every `rekam_medis` read, `persetujuan_pdp` gating cross-faskes data sharing.

### Must NOT have (guardrails, anti-slop, scope boundaries)

- **NEVER build a Flutter application.** No `mobile/` Flutter project is created, and no `pubspec.yaml` with an `sdk: flutter` constraint is added to the repository root. If a `mobile/` directory already exists when execution starts, leave it completely untouched.
- **NEVER hand-write mobile integration documentation when it can be generated.** The OpenAPI spec, the Dart DTOs, and the enum catalogue must all be produced by commands (`sehatly:openapi`, `openapi-typescript`, a Dart generator, `sehatly:enums`) so they cannot drift from the code. Prose supplements the generated artefacts; it does not replace them.
- **NEVER edit, reformat, re-encode, or reorder `telemedicine_test.sql`.** It is read-only law. Extra tables/columns go in *new* migrations and are recorded in `docs/schema-notes.md` as notes (spec §4.4 permits this).
- **NEVER delete, `git checkout`, or overwrite untracked/modified files the plan did not create.** The worktree is dirty (1 commit, ~140 modified, ~60 untracked) and contains uncommitted rebranding work. Wave 0 task 1 baselines it first.
- **NEVER invent endpoints** for the 14 module-orphaned tables: `master_penjamin`, `pasien_penjamin`, `dokter_faskes`, `master_lab_tindakan`, `master_lab_paket`, `lab_paket_item`, `lab_permintaan`, `lab_permintaan_detail`, `lab_hasil`, `klaim_bpjs`, `ulasan_dokter`, `artikel_kategori`, `artikel`, `home_care_pesanan`. Migrations + Models only.
- **NEVER build an `ulasan_dokter` write endpoint**, even though `dokter.rating_rata_rata` is read by M1's `GET /dokter/{id}` — no module in scope creates reviews. Record as a known gap.
- **NEVER add** an SMS provider, real payment-gateway settlement, Agora/Twilio SDK, SATUSEHAT client, or BPJS V-Claim client. Interfaces + working mocks only.
- **NEVER use `socket_io_client`, `echo_dart`, `laravel_echo` (Dart), or the `pusher` broadcast driver.** Reverb/Soketi speak the Pusher protocol; `laravel_echo` Dart ports are 3-5 years stale and `echo_dart` does not exist on pub.dev.
- **NEVER reduce scope** to an MVP, v1, or "phase 1". All five modules are in the request.
- **NEVER rename** any column, type, or FK relation to suit Laravel convenience. If Laravel cannot express something, use raw SQL — not a schema change.
- **NEVER** leave `broader` placeholder/mock data in a client. Both clients hit real endpoints (spec DoD).
- **NEVER** declare `dihapus_at` as anything other than `TIMESTAMP`; use `$table->softDeletes('dihapus_at')`.
- **NEVER** encrypt a value into a column narrower than its ciphertext. `pasien.nik` is `CHAR(16)`; AES-256-CBC ciphertext of a 16-byte plaintext is 32 bytes (44 chars base64 / 64 hex), which does not fit and raises MySQL error 1406 in strict mode. Ciphertext goes in a `TEXT` column; a fixed-width 16-char HMAC carries the unique index. See todo 50.
- **NEVER** describe an ENUM by a count you have not verified against the file. `booking.status` has **8** values, not 7.
- **NEVER** trust an inline `:NNN` citation in this plan over the *Verified SQL line-number index* above — ~30 inline citations were off by one to three before the round-2 sweep, and the index is authoritative.

**Note on the two naming conventions — they are genuinely different and must not be conflated:**

| Table | SOAP column names | Lines |
|---|---|---|
| `konsultasi` | `catatan_subjektif`, `catatan_objektif`, **`catatan_asessment`** (one `s`), `catatan_plan` | 548-551 |

**28 columns carry a reference-shaped name but NO foreign key** 

Using \`constrained()\` on any of these adds a constraint the SQL lacks and breaks parity. This list is **generated from \`telemedicine_test.sql\`**, not hand-maintained, because the previous hand-written version said "Six" while listing eight and omitted at least \`rujukan.icd10_kode\` and \`pasien_riwayat_penyakit.icd10_kode\` ΓÇö both of which had already been correctly implemented as bare columns by an earlier batch, so the omission was survivable only by luck. **Re-derive it if the SQL ever changes; never extend it by hand.**

Bare columns that genuinely reference a table in this schema (25): `artikel.reviewer_user_id`, `audit_log.record_id`, `audit_log.user_id`, `invoice.referensi_id`, `klaim_bpjs.booking_id`, `klaim_bpjs.rekam_medis_id`, `lab_permintaan.konsultasi_id`, `lab_permintaan.rekam_medis_id`, `pasien.kabupaten_kota_id`, `pasien.kecamatan_id`, `pasien.kelurahan_id`, `pasien.provinsi_id`, `pasien_alergi.dicatat_oleh_user_id`, `pasien_penjamin.faskes_rujukan_id`, `pasien_riwayat_penyakit.icd10_kode`, `pasien_tanda_vital.rekam_medis_id`, `rekam_medis_diagnosa.icd10_kode`, `rekam_medis_tindakan.icd9cm_kode`, `resep.konsultasi_id`, `resep.rekam_medis_id`, `rujukan.faskes_asal_id`, `rujukan.icd10_kode`, `surat_keterangan.konsultasi_id`, `ulasan_dokter.konsultasi_id`, `user_devices.device_id`.

Bare columns that are *external* identifiers and must never gain an FK even by accident (3): `faskes.satusehat_org_id`, `konsultasi.room_id`, `rekam_medis.satusehat_encounter_id`. \`konsultasi.room_id\` is a video-SDK room name, \`faskes.satusehat_org_id\` and \`rekam_medis.satusehat_encounter_id\` are SATUSEHAT identifiers ΓÇö all three are strings from systems outside this schema, so a foreign key would be meaningless even though a \`master_*\`-style lookup table might one day exist.

**One exception, and it is the only one:** `pasien_tanda_vital.rekam_medis_id` appears in the list above because the SQL's `CREATE TABLE` omits its constraint — but SQL section `[14]` (`:1161-1163`) adds it as `fk_vital_rm` in migration 76. It is therefore **deferred, not bare**, and it is the sole entry in the *Deferred constraints* registry in `docs/schema-notes.md`. Every other column listed above must stay bare forever. See appendix A.10 and A.11.


**Multi-line ENUMs — treat as single units, not single lines.** Exactly **five** ENUM declarations in the whole contract have a value list that continues onto the following physical line, and reading only the first line yields a **truncated** value list plus a column that looks nullable with no default. Verified by locating every `ENUM(` whose own line lacks its closing paren: `booking.status` (`:515-516`), `master_obat.bentuk_sediaan` (`:713-714`), `resep.status` (`:751-752`), `invoice.status` (`:947-948`), `persetujuan_pdp.jenis` (`:1137-1138`).

**This list was wrong and is now measured rather than asserted. It previously named six entries, of which **two were not wrapped at all** — `konsultasi.status` (`:542`) and `konsultasi_chat.tipe_pesan` (`:568`) each close their own `ENUM(...)` on the same line — while **omitting `invoice.status` (`:947-948`)**, which is genuinely wrapped and belongs to todo 16. An executor reported the true count as **eleven**, naming `lab_permintaan.status` (`:884-885`), `klaim_bpjs.status` (`:1022-1023`), `home_care_pesanan.status` (`:1104-1105`) and `pesanan_obat.status` (`:810-811`) among the rest. **All four of those close their own `ENUM(...)` on the same line**; the following line carries only `NOT NULL DEFAULT ...`. The count is **five**, confirmed by inspecting each candidate line directly. See A.20.

**Confirmed negative:** the file contains **no** `INSERT INTO lab_paket_item` and **no** `INSERT INTO obat_interaksi`. Both tables are created but never seeded, which is why todo 18 reclassifies their rows as unsourced `DevFixtureSeeder` data outside the 1:1 fidelity claim.

**Verified seed row counts** (todo 18 must match exactly): `master_provinsi` 38, `master_agama` 7, `master_golongan_darah` 4, `master_pendidikan` 8, `master_status_pernikahan` 4, `master_hubungan_keluarga` 7, **`master_spesialisasi` 16** (lines 1236-1252), `master_penjamin` 6, **`master_metode_pembayaran` 14**, **`master_icd10` 15**, `master_icd9cm` 6, `master_obat` 7, `master_lab_tindakan` 10, `master_lab_paket` 3, `artikel_kategori` 6.

### Verified schema reality constraints

Every item below was confirmed by direct read of `telemedicine_test.sql` after the Metis gap-analysis pass. They are **not** stylistic preferences — each one is a place where the obvious implementation is wrong, or a spec rule the schema cannot enforce. Implementers must read this list before writing any migration or service.

**`dihapus_at` is `TIMESTAMP`, and `softDeletes()` is the correct call.** `telemedicine_test.sql:148` (`users`) and `:249` (`pasien`) both declare `dihapus_at TIMESTAMP NULL DEFAULT NULL`, and only those two tables have the column. Laravel's `$table->softDeletes('dihapus_at')` emits exactly `timestamp NULL`, so it **matches** the SQL; pair it with `const DELETED_AT = 'dihapus_at'` on the model. Do **not** hand-roll `dateTime('dihapus_at')` — that is the parity break, because it would emit `datetime`. Verified against the SQL and independently by two reviewers.

**Primary-key widths (task 7, 8, 10, 16).** 18 tables do **not** use `BIGINT UNSIGNED` ids: `master_provinsi`/`master_agama`/`master_golongan_darah`/`master_pendidikan`/`master_status_pernikahan`/`master_hubungan_keluarga` are `TINYINT UNSIGNED`; `master_kabupaten_kota`/`master_kecamatan`/`master_spesialisasi`/`roles`/`permissions`/`master_penjamin`/`master_metode_pembayaran`/`artikel_kategori` are `SMALLINT UNSIGNED`; `master_kelurahan` is `MEDIUMINT UNSIGNED`; `master_icd10`/`master_icd9cm` are `INT UNSIGNED`. `$table->id()` is wrong for all of them, and `foreignId()` is wrong for every FK pointing at them (e.g. `pasien.golongan_darah_id` is `TINYINT UNSIGNED`, not `BIGINT UNSIGNED`).
**Non-auto-increment primary keys (task 7, 10, 16).** `master_agama`, `master_golongan_darah`, `master_pendidikan`, `master_status_pernikahan`, `master_hubungan_keluarga` declare `TINYINT UNSIGNED PRIMARY KEY` with **no** `AUTO_INCREMENT`, and the SQL's seed inserts explicit ids. Their migrations must not add auto-increment, their models need `public $incrementing = false`, and their seeders must use `DB::table()->insert()` with explicit ids — Eloquent `create()` would emit a NULL id and fail with MySQL 1366. These five tables also have **no** `dibuat_at`/`diubah_at`.
**Composite and non-incrementing keys (task 19).** `role_permissions`, `user_roles`, `dokter_faskes`, `lab_paket_item` are `(a,b)` composite-PK join tables with no `id` — models need `$incrementing = false` and `protected $primaryKey = ['a','b']`. Seven more tables have a UNIQUE but a surrogate `id`: `user_devices`, `pasien_penjamin`, `dokter_spesialisasi`, `apotek_stok`, `persetujuan_pdp`, `obat_interaksi`, `resep_verifikasi`.
**Timestamp columns are wildly inconsistent (task 19).** Laravel's `$table->timestamps()` must **not** be blanket-applied. Tables with **neither** `dibuat_at` nor `diubah_at`: `master_agama`, `master_golongan_darah`, `master_pendidikan`, `master_status_pernikahan`, `master_hubungan_keluarga`, `master_icd10`, `master_icd9cm`, `roles`, `permissions`, `role_permissions`, `user_roles`, `dokter_spesialisasi`, `dokter_faskes`, `dokter_pendidikan`, `dokter_libur`, `faskes_layanan`, `rekam_medis_diagnosa`, `rekam_medis_tindakan`, `rekam_medis_persetujuan`, `obat_interaksi`, `resep_item`, `resep_verifikasi`, `pesanan_obat_tracking`, `apotek_stok`, `master_lab_tindakan`, `master_lab_paket`, `lab_paket_item`, `lab_permintaan_detail`, `lab_hasil`. Tables with `dibuat_at` only (no `diubah_at`): `user_otp`, `user_devices`, `user_refresh_tokens`, `pasien_anggota_keluarga`, `pasien_alergi`, `pasien_riwayat_penyakit`, `pasien_imunisasi`, `pasien_tanda_vital`, `pasien_penjamin`, `surat_keterangan`, `rujukan`, `rekam_medis_lampiran`, `pembayaran`, `refund`, `notifikasi`, `ulasan_dokter`, `promo_redemption`, `akses_rekam_medis_log`. `konsultasi_chat` has **neither** — its created-at column is `terkirim_at` (`:575`), so its model needs `const CREATED_AT = 'terkirim_at'`. `pesanan_obat_tracking` likewise has only `waktu DATETIME NOT NULL` (`:825`) and no `dibuat_at`. `apotek_stok` has only `diubah_at` (`:837`) and no `dibuat_at`.
**Columns that look like references but have NO foreign key — do not add one (tasks 9, 10, 11, 13, 16, 17).** `surat_keterangan.konsultasi_id` (`:584`), `rujukan.faskes_asal_id` (`:602`), `rekam_medis_lampiran.diunggah_oleh` (`:687`), `lab_hasil.diperiksa_oleh` (`:914`), `pasien_alergi.dicatat_oleh_user_id` (`:281`), `artikel.reviewer_user_id` (`:1078`), `pasien_penjamin.faskes_rujukan_id` (`:346`), and `audit_log.user_id` (`:1120`, deliberately unconstrained so the log survives user deletion). Using `$table->foreignId(...)->constrained(...)` on any of these **adds a constraint the SQL does not have** and is a parity break. `rekam_medis_diagnosa.icd10_kode` (`:660`) and `rekam_medis_tindakan.icd9cm_kode` (`:672`) are likewise bare indexed strings validated only in the application layer.
**Inline `UNIQUE` produces different index names than Laravel (task 6).** MySQL auto-names an inline `UNIQUE` after its column (`users.email` -> index `email`), whereas Laravel's `$table->unique('email')` generates `users_email_unique`. This affects ~25 columns. The parity verifier must therefore compare **uniqueness semantics** (`NON_UNIQUE` flag + ordered column list) and **not** index names, except for the explicitly named keys (`idx_jadwal`, `idx_booking_dokter`, `uq_interaksi`, `uq_stok`, `uq_consent`, `idx_faskes_geo`, `idx_icd10`, `idx_diag_icd10`, `idx_vital_pasien`, `idx_pasien_lahir`, `idx_spesialisasi` etc.), which must match by name. Also note `idx_icd10` is reused as a name on two different tables (`:119` and `:291`), which is legal in MySQL — key the comparison on `(TABLE_NAME, INDEX_NAME)`.
**`CHECK` constraint names are engine-generated (task 17).** `ulasan_dokter` has three inline `CHECK`s (`:1055-1057`) that MySQL names `ulasan_dokter_chk_1/_2/_3` in declaration order. The raw `ALTER TABLE` statements must be issued in the same order to reproduce the same names, and the verifier should compare check *expressions* rather than names.

**Spec rules the schema CANNOT enforce — implement defensively and record in `docs/schema-notes.md`:**
- **Double-booking has no lockable row when `booking.jadwal_id IS NULL`** (`:504` is nullable while `slot_mulai`/`slot_selesai` are `NOT NULL`, `:508-509`). An instant `chat`/`video_call` booking has no `dokter_jadwal` row to `lockForUpdate`, so the task-27 strategy must fall back to locking the `dokter` row (`SELECT ... FROM dokter WHERE id = ? FOR UPDATE`), which exists for every doctor and serialises correctly. **This corrects the draft's earlier claim that `dokter_jadwal` is the only lock target.**
- **Webhook idempotency has no dedupe key.** `pembayaran.nomor_referensi` is `VARCHAR(100) NULL` (`:963`) with **no UNIQUE** and no index; the only index is `idx_bayar_status (status, dibayar_at)` (`:970`). A gateway event id column does not exist. Idempotency must therefore be enforced by an application-level `SELECT ... WHERE nomor_referensi = ? AND gateway = ?` existence check inside the update transaction, accepting the race, plus a unique index **cannot** be added because the SQL is read-only law.
- **PDP consent cannot be revoked.** `UNIQUE KEY uq_consent (user_id, jenis, versi_dokumen)` (`:1144`) with `disetujui TINYINT(1) NOT NULL` (`:1140`) means revoking consent for the same document version would collide with the unique key. Revocation is only representable by inserting a new `versi_dokumen` — semantically wrong but the only option. The consent check must therefore read the **highest** `versi_dokumen` row per `(user_id, jenis)` and honour its `disetujui` value.
- **`resep_verifikasi.resep_id` is `UNIQUE`** (`:788`), so a prescription can be verified exactly once, ever. A rejected prescription therefore **cannot** be revised and re-submitted for verification, and there is no `resep` status for "returned for correction". The rejection path must be documented as terminal.
- **`qr_token` is `NOT NULL` but not `UNIQUE`** on both `surat_keterangan` (`:592`) and `resep` (`:758`). QR verification tokens are security tokens and must be unique. Generate with `Str::uuid()` and add an **application-level** duplicate check (a DB unique index cannot be added).
- **A `sistem` chat message has no sender.** `konsultasi_chat.pengirim_user_id` is `BIGINT UNSIGNED NOT NULL` with a real FK to `users` (`:566`, `:577`), but `pengirim_tipe` includes `'sistem'` (`:568`). System messages must be attributed to a designated service user; create and seed one, and document it.
- **Access-log retention is destroyed by cascade.** `akses_rekam_medis_log.rekam_medis_id` has `ON DELETE CASCADE` (`:1153`), so deleting a medical record deletes the evidence that it was accessed — contrary to UU PDP 27/2022 and Permenkes 24/2022 retention duties. Records must therefore be **never hard-deleted**; only `dihapus_at` soft deletion is permitted, and the cascade must be documented as a known schema defect.
- **Prescription-override decisions are not recordable.** There is no `resep_interaksi` table and no acknowledgement column, so a doctor's decision to prescribe despite a `kontraindikasi` can only be captured as free text in `resep.catatan_dokter` (`:753`). Warnings must be surfaced in the API response and the UI, and the acknowledgement requirement recorded in `docs/schema-notes.md`.
- **Allergy matching has no reliable join key.** `pasien_alergi.nama_alergen` is free text (`:277`), **not** a foreign key to `master_obat`, while `resep_item.nama_obat` is a name *snapshot* (`:771`) and `resep_item.obat_id` is nullable for racikan (`:770`). Allergy checking is therefore best-effort name matching, racikan are structurally uncheckable, and `pasien.catatan_alergi` (`:242`) is a second, unsynchronised source of truth. Document the limitation in the service and in `docs/schema-notes.md`.
- **SOAP is stored twice with no sync rule.** `konsultasi.catatan_subjektif/objektif/asesment/plan` (`:548-551`) and `rekam_medis.subjektif/objektif/asesmen/plan` (`:637-640`) are independent copies. Make `rekam_medis` authoritative and have task 32 write the consultation-level fields as a denormalised copy at completion time; document the direction.
- **Amendment linkage is a convention, not a constraint.** `rekam_medis` has no self-referencing foreign key and no `parent_id`; `konsultasi_id` is nullable. Version chains are reconstructible only when every version shares a non-null `konsultasi_id`. For records with no consultation there is no reliable thread key, and nothing prevents two rows both being `versi = 1, status_dokumen = 'final'`.
- **Prescription expiry has no trigger.** `booking.status` includes `kadaluarsa` but no column records when the payment window closes, so expiry must be computed from `tanggal_kunjungan` + `slot_mulai` in a scheduled command.
- **Queue numbers are unenforced.** `booking.nomor_antrian` is nullable with no per-doctor/day counter and no uniqueness, so duplicate queue numbers are possible.
- **Stock has no movement ledger.** `apotek_stok.jumlah_stok` is mutated in place with no history table, and `jumlah_stok`/`stok_minimum` are **signed** `INT` (`:833-834`) with no `CHECK (jumlah_stok >= 0)`, so negative stock is representable. Guard decrements in the service.
- **Line items cannot be recorded for over-the-counter orders.** `pesanan_obat` (`:797-817`) has `resep_id` nullable and **no** `pesanan_obat_item` table, so a `tipe = 'obat_bebas'` order has no place to record which products were bought and `subtotal` cannot be recomputed from lines. Restrict task 46 to `tipe = 'resep_dokter'` and record the limitation.
- **Seed data contains no domain fixtures.** SQL section `[16]` seeds only master data — **no** `users`, `dokter`, `pasien`, `faskes`, `dokter_jadwal`, or `booking` rows. Therefore `v_dokter_katalog` (`:1170`) is **empty** after a plain `migrate --seed`, and M1's `GET /dokter` would return nothing. Task 18 must add a `DevFixtureSeeder` (clearly separated from the SQL-derived seeders, and documented as not part of the 1:1 claim) creating at least one verified doctor with schedule, one patient, one apotek `faskes`, and stock.
- **Partial slot unavailability is inexpressible.** `dokter_libur` is whole-day only (`:490-496`), with no unique on `(dokter_id, tanggal)` and no index beyond the FK, so a single blocked slot on a specific date cannot be represented and holiday lookups scan.
- **`v_dokter_katalog` truncates.** `GROUP_CONCAT` (`:1178`) is capped by `group_concat_max_len` (default 1024), silently truncating the specialisation list for a doctor with many specialisations.
- **`v_pendapatan_bulanan` groups by server-local wall clock.** `DATE_FORMAT(p.dibayar_at, '%Y-%m')` (`:1191`) groups an unzoned `DATETIME` while the API contract mandates UTC — a third instance of the timezone duality that `docs/timezone-policy.md` must cover.
- **`dokter_jadwal` permits self-overlap and out-of-range weekdays.** `hari` is `TINYINT UNSIGNED` with no `CHECK (hari BETWEEN 0 AND 6)` and no constraint preventing two active rows for the same `(dokter_id, hari)` with overlapping `jam_mulai`/`jam_selesai`. Validate in `SlotAvailabilityService` and document.
- **Pharmacy identity is not constrained.** `resep.apotek_id` (`:749`), `apotek_stok.apotek_id` (`:838`) and `pesanan_obat.apotek_id` (`:816`) all reference `faskes(id)` with no `tipe = 'apotek'` constraint, so the application must validate it.
- **Doctor visibility requires verified STR.** `v_dokter_katalog` filters `status_verifikasi = 'terverifikasi'` (`:1183`), and nothing automatically invalidates that when `dokter.str_berlaku_sampai` (`:414`) passes. The directory service must check the STR date too.
- **`MySQL TIME` permits values beyond 24:00**, so a slot spanning midnight is representable but will break naive `H:i:s` parsing. `SlotAvailabilityService` must reject or explicitly handle a `jam_selesai <= jam_mulai` wrap.
**ALWAYS** use `softDeletes('dihapus_at')` — see the `dihapus_at` entry under *Verified schema reality constraints* above for the verified SQL line numbers.

## Verification strategy
> Zero human intervention - all verification is agent-executed.
- Test decision: **tests-after** for migrations, models, resources, and CRUD; **TDD** for the four high-risk domain engines (`SlotAvailabilityService` + booking lock, `ObatInteraksiService`, `RekamMedisService` versioning/amendment, payment-webhook idempotency). Framework: **Pest 4** + `pest-plugin-laravel` (already pinned in `composer.json:26-28`, already bootstrapped in `tests/Pest.php:17-19`).
- Database under test: **MySQL 8**, `telemedisin_db_test`, `RefreshDatabase`. Not SQLite — the schema is MySQL-8-only.
- Schema parity: `php artisan sehatly:verify-schema` diffs live `information_schema` (columns, types, unsigned, nullability, defaults, extra, indexes, FKs, CHECKs) against a parser of `telemedicine_test.sql`, and exits non-zero on any drift.
- Mobile-contract verification: the Dart package in `packages/sehatly_api_client/` is validated by `dart test` (pure Dart, no Flutter SDK needed) against mocked HTTP; the OpenAPI spec is validated by a drift check against the live route table; and `docs/enums.json` is validated against `information_schema.COLUMNS` so no ENUM list can be stale.
- Static analysis gates per wave: `php artisan test`, `vendor/bin/pint --test`, `npm run types:check` + `npm run build` (web), `dart analyze` + `dart test` (Dart package).
- Evidence: `.omo/evidence/` (outside ulw-loop). One file per todo: `.omo/evidence/task-<N>-sehatly.md`.

## Execution strategy

### Parallel execution waves
> Target 5-8 todos per wave. Fewer than 3 (except the final) means you under-split.

- **Wave 0 — Foundation & de-risking (6 todos).** Baseline the dirty worktree, repair the `laravel/passkeys` composer drift, flip to MySQL 8, stand up the API kernel and RBAC kernel, create the `web/` SPA skeleton, and build the schema-parity verifier. **Everything else is blocked on this wave.**
- **Wave 1 — Schema core (6 todos).** Migration batches A-F: master data, users/RBAC, patient, facility/doctor, schedule/booking, consultation.
- **Wave 2 — Schema commerce & compliance (6 todos).** Migration batches G-L: medical record, pharmacy, laboratory, billing, content/audit, deferred FK + views.
- **Wave 3 — Module 1 backend (4 todos: 19-22).** Base model trait + models, auth endpoints, profile/family/allergy endpoints, doctor directory.
- **Wave 4 — Module 1 web + first mobile-contract slice (3 todos).** Web auth+OTP+profile screens; the pure-Dart API client package (auth lifecycle, refresh, storage split); Module 1 summary.
- **Wave 5 — Module 2 (5 todos).** Slot service, booking endpoints, web calendar, the Dart realtime + push integration package, summary + Inertia removal.
- **Wave 6 — Module 3 (7 todos: 31-37).** Reverb plumbing, consultation+chat, medical record service, referral letters, web chat/SOAP, the mobile integration guide, summary.
- **Wave 7 — Module 4 (5 todos).** Interaction/allergy engine, e-prescribe, verification+history, web composer, reference-data endpoints + enum catalogue.
- **Wave 8 — Module 5 (7 todos).** Audit observers, invoice service, payment gateway + webhook, checkout + stock, PDP consent + notifications, web checkout, contract-conformance suite.
- **Wave 9 — Cross-cutting hardening (5 todos).** NIK encryption, timezone policy, throttling, OpenAPI spec + generated client types + README, final full-suite run.

> **CHECKPOINT MANUAL WAJIB SETELAH WAVE 2 (skema selesai).** Setelah todo 18 selesai, jalankan `php artisan migrate:fresh --seed` lalu `php artisan sehatly:verify-schema`, dan **tunjukkan hasil verbatim kepada pengguna untuk konfirmasi eksplisit sebelum Wave 3 (Modul 1) dimulai.** Ini **bukan** gerbang otomatis dan **tidak** boleh dilewati oleh executor: kesalahan di lapisan skema akan menjalar ke kelima modul di atasnya, dan 5 dari 54 todo bergantung padanya. Executor WAJIB berhenti dan menunggu di sini. Pendant: masih ada ~35 referensi nomor baris `telemedicine_test.sql` yang belum diverifikasi (lihat `docs/schema-notes.md`), sehingga hasil verifikasi harus dibaca terhadap sumber, bukan hanya dianggap lulus/gagal.
>
> **Scope dan risiko tetap XL/High — tidak direvisi.** Jumlah todo tetap 54. Resiko utama tidak berubah: konversi destruktif aplikasi live dengan worktree kotor, plus 75 tabel yang harus cocok persis.

**Migration filename discipline (load-bearing for parallelism).** Migration *N* handles SQL table *N*, using filename `2026_10_01_NNNNNN_<table>_table.php` where `NNNNNN` is the zero-padded index from the table-order table in Wave 1 task 7. This gives (a) deterministic, SQL-matching FK ordering and (b) collision-free parallel authoring — multiple agents may write batches concurrently because no two ever pick the same filename. The mapping table is the single source of truth and **must be created in Wave 1 task 7 before any batch is written**.

### Dependency matrix
> Per-todo `Parallelization` lines are authoritative; this table is the wave-level view.
| Wave | Todos | Gate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 Foundation | 1-6 | nothing may start before the worktree is baselined (todo 1) and the verifier exists (todo 6) |
| 1 Schema core | 7-12 | strict chain 7->8->9->10->11->12; migration N handles SQL table N so FK order is never violated |
| 2 Schema commerce | 13-18 | 13 -> {14,15} -> 16 -> 17 -> 18; 18 is terminal for the schema |
| 3 Module 1 backend | 19-22 | 19 is the model foundation; 20 -> 21; 22 parallel with 21 |
| 4 Module 1 web + Dart SDK core | 23-25 | 24 (Dart client) parallel with 23 (web); 25 (summary) last |
| 5 Module 2 | 26-30 | 26 -> 27 -> {28,29} -> 30; 29 (Dart realtime) needs 24+31; 30 also strips Inertia |
| 6 Module 3 | 31-37 | 31 (Reverb) -> 32 -> {33,34} -> {35,36} -> 37; 36 is the mobile guide || 7 Module 4 | 38-42 | 38 -> 39 -> 40 -> {41,42}; 42 publishes enums + referensi endpoints |
| 8 Module 5 | 43-49 | 43 -> 44 -> 45 -> 46 -> 47 -> {48,49}; 44 parallel with 43; 49 is contract conformance |
| 9 Hardening | 50-54 | {50,51,52} parallel -> 53 -> 54 |
| Final | F1-F4 | all four run in parallel after 54; all must approve |

- [x] 6. Build the schema-parity verifier and record the schema-notes log
  What to do / Must NOT do: Create `app/Console/Commands/VerifySchemaParity.php` (`php artisan sehatly:verify-schema`). It must: (a) parse `telemedicine_test.sql` into a normalised expected model — table name, column name, MySQL type string, unsigned flag, nullability, default, extra, inline `INDEX`/`UNIQUE KEY`/`PRIMARY KEY`, `FOREIGN KEY` target + `ON DELETE` action, and `CHECK` constraints; (b) query live `information_schema.COLUMNS`, `.STATISTICS`, `.REFERENTIAL_CONSTRAINTS`, `.TABLE_CONSTRAINTS`, `.VIEWS` for the configured database; (c) diff the two and print a table of every discrepancy; (d) `exit(1)` if any discrepancy exists, `exit(0)` only on an exact match. Use `SHOW CREATE TABLE` as the authoritative live source and normalise both sides (strip backticks, collapse whitespace, lowercase type names, expand `int(11)`-style display widths) so cosmetic differences are not false positives — but a real type/unsigned/null/default/index/FK/CHECK difference MUST be reported. Also create `docs/schema-notes.md` as the running log of every table or column that exists in the migrations but NOT in `telemedicine_test.sql` (first entries: Sanctum's `personal_access_tokens`, Laravel's `cache`/`cache_locks`/`jobs`/`job_batches`/`failed_jobs`, and the `migrations` table), each with a one-line justification. This is the mechanism that lets the plan add infrastructure without editing the SQL file (spec §4.4). Must NOT auto-create or auto-alter anything — the command is read-only and diagnostic. Must NOT import the SQL file into the database.

This file is the single source of truth for the whole schema effort and **must land before any other batch is written**, because it is what makes parallel batch authoring collision-free. The order is: 1 `master_provinsi`(:58) 2 `master_kabupaten_kota`(:64) 3 `master_kecamatan`(:72) 4 `master_kelurahan`(:80) 5 `master_agama`(:89) 6 `master_golongan_darah`(:94) 7 `master_pendidikan`(:99) 8 `master_status_pernikahan`(:104) 9 `master_hubungan_keluarga`(:109) 10 `master_icd10`(:115) 11 `master_icd9cm`(:122). Then author those 11 migrations. Parity rules that apply to every migration in the plan: `id` uses the exact unsigned width from the SQL (`TINYINT UNSIGNED`, `SMALLINT UNSIGNED`, `MEDIUMINT UNSIGNED`, `BIGINT UNSIGNED`) — **never** `$table->id()`, which emits `BIGINT UNSIGNED` and breaks `master_provinsi`/`master_agama`/etc.; `$table->enum('nama', [...])` with values in the SQL's exact order; `$table->json()` for JSON columns; `->index(['col'])` / `->unique([...])` declared explicitly rather than relying on `constrained()` shorthand, because several SQL FKs have **no** matching index (`users.email` UNIQUE yes, but `faskes.kode_faskes` UNIQUE nullable at `:363`); `$table->year('tahun_terdiagnosis')`; and for every `dibuat_at`/`diubah_at`, declare `->useCurrent()` and issue a follow-up raw `DB::statement('ALTER TABLE x MODIFY diubah_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP')` inside the same migration, because Laravel 13 has **no Blueprint helper for `ON UPDATE CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`**. Note `master_icd10` has a redundant `INDEX idx_icd10 (kode)` alongside `UNIQUE (kode)` (`:119`) — reproduce both. - [x] 8. Migration batch B (users, RBAC, OTP, devices, refresh tokens — SQL tables 12-19)
  What to do / Must NOT do: Author migrations 12-19: `users`(:132) `roles`(:151) `permissions`(:157) `role_permissions`(:163) `user_roles`(:171) `user_otp`(:179) `user_devices`(:190) `user_refresh_tokens`(:204). `users` parity is the critical one and differs from the current scaffold migration in almost every column: `uuid CHAR(36) NOT NULL UNIQUE`, `nama_lengkap VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL`, `email VARCHAR(255) NULL UNIQUE` (nullable — MySQL permits many NULLs in a UNIQUE index, which is what lets a patient register with phone only), `no_telepon VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL UNIQUE`, `kata_sandi_hash VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL`, `tipe ENUM('pasien','dokter','perawat','apoteker','kurir','admin','superadmin') DEFAULT 'pasien'`, `status ENUM('pending_verifikasi','aktif','nonaktif','ditangguhkan') DEFAULT 'pending_verifikasi'`, `foto_profil VARCHAR(500) NULL`, `bahasa ENUM('id','en') DEFAULT 'id'`, `telepon_terverifikasi TINYINT(1) DEFAULT 0`, `email_terverifikasi TINYINT(1) DEFAULT 0`, `last_login_at DATETIME NULL`, `dibuat_at`/`diubah_at` TIMESTAMP with `ON UPDATE`, `dihapus_at TIMESTAMP NULL DEFAULT NULL` — declare it with **`$table->softDeletes('dihapus_at')`**, which emits exactly `timestamp NULL` and therefore matches the SQL; do **not** substitute a hand-rolled `dateTime('dihapus_at')`, which would introduce the parity break. The scaffold's `name`/`email`/`email_verified_at`/`password`/`remember_token` columns must be **absent**. `role_permissions` and `user_roles` are pure composite-PK join tables with **no** `id` column and `PRIMARY KEY (a,b)` — do not add an id. `user_otp.kode_hash VARCHAR(255)` stores a **hash**, never the plaintext OTP; note that the schema has **no** attempt-counter column, so OTP brute-force protection is application-only and lives entirely in the task-20 rate limiter. `user_refresh_tokens` has **no** unique on `token_hash` (`:207`) and no `device_id` column, so (a) every refresh is a full table scan and (b) per-device token revocation is impossible — implement rotation with an application-level existence check and record both limitations in `docs/schema-notes.md`. Drop the scaffold-only migrations `2024_01_01_000000_create_passkeys_table.php` and `2025_08_14_170933_add_two_factor_columns_to_users_table.php`, and the `password_reset_tokens` and `sessions` tables created by `0001_01_01_000000_create_users_table.php:24-37` (neither is in the SQL, and password reset goes through `user_otp.tujuan = 'reset_kata_sandi'` at `:182`) — but only after task 1's baseline commit exists. Finally, `users.kata_sandi_hash` is `NOT NULL` with no nullable or OTP-only representation, so an OTP-only signup must still generate a random unusable hash; document that. Must NOT yet rewrite `app/Models/User.php` (task 19) — but DO delete the old `create_users_table` migration once the new `users` migration is in place, in the same commit, so the tree is never in a state with two conflicting `users` migrations.

Create `app/Services/Auth/OtpSender.php` as an **interface** with a `LogOtpSender` implementation writing to `storage/logs/laravel.log` and returning the code in the response **only** when `app()->environment('local')` — the real SMS provider is out of scope. Create `app/Services/Auth/TokenService.php` issuing the Sanctum access token via `$user->createToken($deviceName, abilities, $expiresAt)` and a refresh token stored as a SHA-256 hash in `user_refresh_tokens` (`:206` — same rule as OTP) with rotation: inside a transaction, `lockForUpdate()` the `user_refresh_tokens` row matching the presented hash **before** reading `dicabut` (Oracle finding — without the lock, two concurrent refreshes both read `dicabut = 0`, both mint a new token, and the reuse detector is defeated, which defeats the entire point of rotation); then assert `dicabut = 0` and `kedaluwarsa_at > now`, mark it `dicabut = 1`, and issue a new one. **Reject reuse of an already-revoked refresh token** (that is the theft signal) and, because `user_refresh_tokens` has no `device_id` column to scope revocation, revoke **every** active token for that user on reuse detection before returning 401; record that per-device revocation is impossible in this schema. Endpoints, all under `routes/api.php` with `Route::prefix('auth')`: `POST /auth/register` creates `users` with `status = 'pending_verifikasi'` and `tipe = 'pasien'` plus a `pasien` row, then sends an OTP with `tujuan = 'verifikasi_telepon'`. **The `pasien` row cannot be created empty** (Oracle finding): `jenis_kelamin ENUM('L','P') NOT NULL` (`:225`), `tanggal_lahir DATE NOT NULL` (`:226`) and `alamat_lengkap TEXT NOT NULL` (`:234`) all have **no default**, so under MySQL 8's default `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` an insert omitting any of them fails with error 1364 "Field doesn't have a default value". `StoreRegisterRequest` must therefore require `jenis_kelamin`, `tanggal_lahir`, and `alamat_lengkap` on the register call, and the web/mobile register forms must collect them — or, if a minimal sign-up is wanted, create the `users` row first and defer the `pasien` row to `PUT /pasien/profil`; pick the **former** (collect at register) and state it, because a patient with no `pasien` row cannot book, and every downstream query joins through `pasien`. `POST /auth/login` accepts **either** `no_telepon` **or** `email` plus `password`, throttled, and sends an OTP with `tujuan = 'login'` — it must **not** return a token; `POST /auth/otp/verify` checks the hash, `kedaluwarsa_at`, and `sudah_dipakai = 0`, then flips `telepon_terverifikasi = 1`, `status = 'aktif'`, `last_login_at`, marks the OTP used, and returns access + refresh; `POST /auth/refresh` rotates; `POST /auth/logout` revokes the current Sanctum token via `$request->user()->currentAccessToken()->delete()`, the supplied refresh token, **and sets `user_devices.aktif = 0` for every device belonging to that user** (Oracle finding — `user_devices.aktif` exists at `:197` and nothing else in the plan ever writes it, so without this a logged-out device keeps receiving that user's medical push notifications: prescription ready, new chat message, payment settled). Add `DELETE /api/v1/auth/devices/{deviceId}` so a single device can be revoked, and have `POST /auth/devices` set `aktif = 1` again on re-registration; `POST /auth/devices` upserts into `user_devices` on the `UNIQUE KEY uq_device (user_id, device_id)` (`:200`) with `platform ENUM('android','ios','web')`, `fcm_token`, `app_versi`, and touches `last_active_at`. Every endpoint returns the task-3 envelope. Add `routes/api.php` rate limiters: 5/min on login, 5/min on OTP verify, 10/min on OTP send. Must NOT return `kata_sandi_hash` anywhere, must NOT return the OTP in a non-local environment, must NOT allow a token before OTP verification.
  Parallelization: Wave 3 | Blocked by: 3, 4, 19 | Blocks: 21, 22, 23 | Can parallelize with: none

  Acceptance criteria (agent-executable): `php artisan route:list --path=api/v1/auth` lists all 6 routes; a feature test walks register -> OTP verify -> authenticated `GET /me` and asserts the envelope shape at each step; a test asserts `user_otp.kode_hash` !== the plaintext code; a test asserts a second verify attempt with the same code returns 422; a test asserts an expired OTP (`kedaluwarsa_at` in the past) returns 422; a test asserts login alone returns **no** `data.token`; a test asserts refresh rotates the row (`dicabut = 1` on the old) and that replaying the old refresh token returns 401.

  What to do / Must NOT do: Create `packages/sehatly_api_client/`, a **pure-Dart** package (`sdk: '>=3.13.0 <4.0.0'`, **no** `flutter:` SDK constraint, so it is testable without the Flutter SDK and importable from any Flutter app). (b) `TokenStore` — an **abstract** interface with a `SecureTokenStore` implementation delegating to `flutter_secure_storage` 11.2.0 and a `PlainTokenStore` for non-secret values, so the package stays Flutter-free while documenting the secure default; the package's README must state the OWASP MASVS-STORAGE-1 rationale for the split and note that a pure-Dart package cannot import `flutter_secure_storage` directly, so the concrete implementation lives in the consuming Flutter app behind this interface; (c) `RefreshTokenStore` with the same split, and explicit handling of the **rotation + reuse-detection** contract from task 20 — the client must treat a 401 on `/auth/refresh` as "session unrecoverable", clear both stores, and emit a `sessionExpired` callback, because the server revokes the presented refresh token on use; (d) strongly-typed `ApiException` carrying `statusCode`, `message`, and the `errors` map, parsed from the task-3 error envelope, with helpers `isValidationError`, `isUnauthorized`, `isForbidden`, `isSlotTaken` (matched on the `slot` error key from task 27), `isConsentRequired` (403 with the PDP message from task 47); (e) `Paginated<T>` parsing the `meta: {current_page, last_page, total}` block; (f) hand-written DTOs for the envelope, auth, doctor, patient, booking, slot, consultation, chat message, medical record, prescription, prescription item, drug warning, invoice, payment, medicine order, tracking, and notification shapes, regenerated from the OpenAPI spec in task 53 and committed alongside it; (g) typed endpoint classes (`AuthApi`, `DokterApi`, `BookingApi`, `KonsultasiApi`, `ResepApi`, `PesananApi`, `NotifikasiApi`, `ReferensiApi`) so the mobile team's call sites read `await client.booking.create(...)` rather than hand-built paths; (h) a `FakeSehatlyClient` for the mobile team's widget tests. Write `packages/sehatly_api_client/README.md` with a copy-pasteable `pubspec.yaml` dependency block, a 20-line bootstrap example, and the base-URL/environment table. Add `dart test` unit tests for the interceptor, the error parser, the pagination parser, and the refresh single-flight behaviour. Must NOT depend on Flutter, must NOT put tokens in a plain store by default, must NOT retry a non-idempotent request after refresh without an explicit opt-out flag.

- [x] 26. Implement `SlotAvailabilityService` and the doctor schedule/slot endpoints
  What to do / Must NOT do: **TDD this one.** Create `app/Services/Booking/SlotAvailabilityService.php`. `getJadwal($dokterId)` returns the weekly schedule grouped by `hari` (0=Minggu..6=Sabtu, `:474`) with `tipe_layanan`, `jam_mulai`/`jam_selesai`, `durasi_slot_menit`, and `faskes`. `getSlotTerbuka($dokterId, string $tanggal)` implements the spec's subtraction exactly: take the `dokter_jadwal` rows for that `hari` where `status_aktif = 1` and `berlaku_mulai <= $tanggal` and (`berlaku_sampai IS NULL` or `>= $tanggal`); **subtract** every date in `dokter_libur` for that doctor; generate candidate slots by stepping `durasi_slot_menit` from `jam_mulai` to `jam_selesai`; then **subtract** slots overlapping any `booking` whose `status NOT IN ('dibatalkan','kadaluarsa')` — and here is the trap the schema creates: a slot is free only when the count of overlapping active bookings is **less than** `dokter_jadwal.kuota_per_sesi`, defaulting to `1` when that column is `NULL`. A naive `count == 0` check is a bug that makes `kuota_per_sesi > 1` schedules permanently unbookable. Also mark slots in the past as unavailable when `$tanggal` is today. Endpoints: `GET /api/v1/dokter/{dokter}/jadwal` (public) and `GET /api/v1/dokter/{dokter}/slot?tanggal=YYYY-MM-DD` (public), both returning `{tanggal, timezone: 'Asia/Jakarta', slots: [{jam_mulai, jam_selesai, tipe_layanan, faskes_id, tersedia, alasan}]}`. `tanggal` must be validated with `date_format:Y-m-d` and rejected with 422 if malformed. Document in a class docblock that `$tanggal`, `slot_mulai`, and `slot_selesai` are **Asia/Jakarta wall-clock** and are never timezone-converted, per `docs/timezone-policy.md`. Must NOT create or modify `dokter_jadwal` — the spec lists no write endpoint for it — so seed schedules via a `DokterJadwalSeeder` in tests/factories.

- [x] 27. Implement booking create, list, cancel, and the doctor-side list
  What to do / Must NOT do: `POST /api/v1/booking` validates `dokter_id`, optional `jadwal_id`, `tipe_layanan ENUM('chat','video_call','kunjungan_klinik','home_visit')`, `tanggal_kunjungan`, `slot_mulai`, `keluhan`, `lampiran_keluhan` (array of `{nama, url}`), optional `anggota_keluarga_id` (validated to belong to the caller), and `is_rujukan`/`is_konsultasi_lanjutan` booleans. **Double-booking prevention — the load-bearing part, and the first design was WRONG (Oracle finding).** The SQL has **no** unique index on `(dokter_id, tanggal_kunjungan, slot_mulai)` (only `idx_booking_dokter` at `:527`), and the SQL is read-only law, so the constraint route is unavailable. **Use ONE serialization point for every booking type: always `lockForUpdate()` the `dokter` row first** (`Dokter::whereKey($dokterId)->lockForUpdate()->firstOrFail()`), acquired as the **first** statement of the transaction. The `dokter` row exists for every doctor regardless of `tipe_layanan`, and it is the narrowest resource that both the scheduled and the instant path contend for. An earlier draft locked `dokter_jadwal` when `jadwal_id` was present and `dokter` otherwise — **that is a double-booking hole**: a scheduled request and an instant request for the same doctor and overlapping time would lock *different rows*, run concurrently, and both observe a count below quota. (`booking.jadwal_id` is `BIGINT UNSIGNED NULL` at `:504` while `slot_mulai`/`slot_selesai` are `NOT NULL` at `:508-509`, so instant bookings are legal and the two paths really do coexist.) If `jadwal_id` is supplied, lock the `dokter_jadwal` row **second**, after `dokter` — never before — so lock ordering is globally consistent and deadlock-free. Then run the overlap count with `lockForUpdate()` on the query itself: `where('dokter_id',…)->where('tanggal_kunjungan',…)->whereNotIn('status', ['dibatalkan','kadaluarsa'])->where('slot_mulai','<',$slotSelesai)->where('slot_selesai','>',$slotMulai)` compared against `kuota_per_sesi ?? 1`, throwing `SlotTakenException` (422) when exhausted, all inside the same `DB::transaction`. **The overlap query MUST itself use `lockForUpdate()`, not a plain `count()`.** Under MySQL's default REPEATABLE READ a plain `SELECT` is a *consistent read* against a snapshot while `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` is a *current read*; relying on "the parent lock already blocked the competitor" is correct only while no earlier consistent read has fixed the snapshot, which is one `->first()` refactor away from silently breaking. Because the `dokter` row is already locked, no competing transaction can be inserting a conflicting booking, so the extra row locks are uncontended. State this reasoning in a code comment so it is not "simplified" away later. The overlap predicate is strict, so back-to-back bookings do not conflict. All cases then run the same overlap count: `where('dokter_id',…)->where('tanggal_kunjungan',…)->whereNotIn('status', ['dibatalkan','kadaluarsa'])->where('slot_mulai','<',$slotSelesai)->where('slot_selesai','>',$slotMulai)` compared against `kuota_per_sesi ?? 1`, throwing `SlotTakenException` (422) when exhausted — all inside one `DB::transaction`. **The overlap query MUST itself use `lockForUpdate()`, not a plain `count()`.** Reason: MySQL's default isolation is REPEATABLE READ, where a plain `SELECT` is a *consistent read* against a snapshot, while `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` is a *current read* against the latest committed data. Relying on "the `FOR UPDATE` blocked until the competitor committed, so the later plain count sees it" is correct only if no earlier consistent read already fixed the snapshot in this transaction — a fragile invariant that a future refactor adding a `->first()` lookup would silently break. Making the count a current read too makes the check correct under **any** isolation level. Because the parent row (`dokter_jadwal` or `dokter`) is already locked, no competing transaction can be inserting a conflicting booking at that moment, so the extra row locks are uncontended and deadlock-free. State this reasoning in a code comment so it is not "simplified" away later. The overlap predicate is strict, so back-to-back bookings do not conflict. Set `status = 'menunggu_pembayaran'`, generate `nomor_booking` as `BK` + `Ymd` + a 6-character sequence, **retrying up to 3 times on a `QueryException` for the `nomor_booking` UNIQUE collision before returning 422** (Oracle finding: the column is `VARCHAR(30) NOT NULL UNIQUE`, so a collision under concurrency is otherwise an unhandled 500), and set `dibuat_oleh_user_id`. Per the spec, `is_rujukan`/`is_konsukasi_lanjutan` **do not** apply any automatic discount — encode that as an explicit code comment so it is not "helpfully" changed later. `nomor_antrian` is nullable with no uniqueness and no counter, so leave it `null` rather than inventing a collision-prone sequence, and record that in `docs/schema-notes.md`. Also create the `Invoice` for the booking via task 44's `InvoiceService` (status `menunggu_pembayaran`, `referensi_tipe = 'booking'`). `GET /api/v1/pasien/booking` lists the caller's bookings filtered by `?status=`, paginated, with `dokter` and `jadwal` eager-loaded. `PUT /api/v1/booking/{id}/batalkan` sets `status = 'dibatalkan'`, `dibatalkan_oleh` (from `users.tipe`, so a doctor cancelling yields `'dokter'`), `alasan_pembatalan`, and cancels the linked invoice — and must reject cancelling a booking already in `selesai` or `berlangsung` with 422. `GET /api/v1/dokter/booking` lists bookings for the authenticated doctor's `dokter` row, guarded by `tipe:dokter`, filtered by `?tanggal=&status=`. Also **return an unmasked-NIK prohibition test from every endpoint that eager-loads `pasien`** (Oracle finding): success criterion 4 states no response may contain an unmasked NIK, but the plan only enforced it on `/me`, while `BookingResource`, `KonsultasiResource`, `ResepResource`, `PesananObatResource`, and `RekamMedisResource` all eager-load `pasien`. Add a single data-driven test that iterates every registered `/api/v1` GET route, calls it as a patient and as the owning doctor, and asserts the serialised body contains no key matching `/nik/i` with an unmasked 16-digit value — so a future Resource that forgets `NikMasker` fails the build. Create `App\Http\Requests\{StoreBookingRequest, CancelBookingRequest}` and `App\Http\Resources\BookingResource`. - [x] 29. Build the Dart realtime + push integration package for the mobile team
  What to do / Must NOT do: Extend `packages/sehatly_api_client/` with a `RealtimeClient` and a push-registration helper, because realtime is the second-hardest thing to get right from prose. `RealtimeClient` wraps `laravel_reverb: ^0.7.1` (the actively published pure-Dart Pusher-protocol client) — **not** `socket_io_client`, because Reverb and Soketi speak the Pusher protocol, and **not** any Laravel Echo Dart port (`echo_dart` does not exist on pub.dev; `laravel_echo` and friends are 3-5 years stale). The wrapper must: `Reverb(host:, port:, appKey:, useTls:, authEndpoint: '/api/broadcasting/auth', authHeaders: () async => {'Authorization': 'Bearer $accessToken', 'Accept': 'application/json'})` — the token must be read from the `TokenStore` **at subscribe time**, not cached at construction, because the access token rotates and a stale token makes channel authorisation fail; subscribe with `reverb.private('kons Boscoultasi.$id')` (the client library adds the `private-` wire prefix itself) and listen on the `chat.pesan` event name from task 31; expose a `Stream<ChatMessage>`; on `onReconnected`, invoke a caller-supplied `onResync` callback, because **the broker does not replay missed messages** — a gap that must be handled explicitly or messages are silently lost on reconnect; and de-duplicate by message id against the REST history, exactly as the web client does in task 35. Add `PushRegistration` with an FCM flow: request permission, call `getToken()`, POST it to `POST /api/v1/auth/devices` with `platform: 'android'|'ios'`, re-register on `onTokenRefresh`, and handle `onMessageOpenedApp` deep links. Because the package is Flutter-free, the FCM calls go behind a `PushTokenProvider` interface with the `firebase_messaging` wiring supplied as a **copy-pasteable code block in the README** rather than compiled here — and the README must call out the two platform traps verbatim: on iOS the app is a UIScene app so `AppDelegate.didFinishLaunchingWithOptions` must call `FLTFirebaseMessagingPlugin.configureNotificationCenterDelegate()` as the **first** statement **before** `super`, and on Android the manifest needs `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` plus a `com.google.firebase.messaging.default_notification_channel_id` meta-data entry. Add `dart test` coverage for channel-name construction, the `authHeaders` token freshness (assert the token is re-read on each subscribe), the reconnect `onResync` invocation, and message de-duplication. Must NOT use Socket.IO, must NOT cache the token at construction, must NOT assume replay after reconnect, must NOT compile Flutter plugin code inside the package.

- [x] 32. Implement consultation lifecycle and chat endpoints
  What to do / Must NOT do: `POST /api/v1/konsultasi/mulai` accepts either `booking_id` (must be the caller's, and its `status` must be `terjadwal` or `check_in`) **or** the instant "Tanya Dokter" form with `dokter_id` + `tipe` and **no** `booking_id` — which works because `konsultasi.booking_id` is `NULL UNIQUE` (`:539`), so unlimited instant consultations are representable. It sets `status = 'menunggu_dokter'`, generates `room_id` as a `Str::uuid()` for the video SDK, and returns the consultation. For the instant flow, pick the doctor from `dokter` where `status_verifikasi = 'terverifikasi' AND tersedia_telemedisin = 1`, and record the fee from `biaya_konsultasi_online`. `GET /api/v1/konsultasi/{id}` returns the session plus `pasien`, `dokter`, `booking`, the four SOAP fields, and a `total_durasi_detik` computed value; it must be scoped so only the session's patient, its doctor, or an `admin`/`superadmin` can read it. `POST /api/v1/konsultasi/{id}/chat` accepts `isi` (required for `tipe_pesan = 'teks'`) or a file upload (for `gambar`/`dokumen`/`audio`/`video_note`, stored on the `public` disk with `file_url`/`file_nama`/`file_ukuran_kb`), or one of the system types `resep`/`surat_keterangan`/`sistem`; it infers `pengirim_tipe` from `users.tipe` and dispatches `KonsultasiMessageSent` from task 31. `GET /api/v1/konsultasi/{id}/chat` returns history paginated **ascending** by `terkirim_at` (chat reads oldest-first, unlike every other list in the system) with `per_page` capped at 100, and a separate `POST /konsultasi/{id}/chat/baca` that stamps `dibaca_at` on the other party's unread messages. `PUT /api/v1/konsultasi/{id}/selesai` is **doctor-only** (`tipe:dokter` + an ownership check), sets `status = 'selesai'`, `selesai_at`, `total_durasi_detik` from `mulai_at`, and writes the four SOAP fields plus `diagnosis_kerja` and `saran_tindak_lanjut`; it must be rejected with 422 if `mulai_at` is still null, and must be idempotent-safe (a second call returns 422, not a double-write). Create `App\Http\Requests\{MulaiKonsultasiRequest, KirimPesanRequest, SelesaikanKonsultasiRequest}` and `App\Http\Resources\{KonsultasiResource, KonsultasiChatResource}`. Must NOT let a patient set `status`, `mulai_at`, or `selesai_at`, must NOT let a non-owner read the chat, must NOT use `ShouldBroadcast`.

- [ ] 36. Write `docs/mobile-integration.md` — the human-readable guide for the Flutter team
  What to do / Must NOT do: Write the single document the mobile team reads first, `docs/mobile-integration.md`. It must be concrete enough that an engineer who has never seen this codebase can integrate without asking a question. Required sections, in order: (1) **Prerequisites and environment** — base URLs per environment, the requirement that the API be reachable over HTTPS for release builds, and the note that the mobile app must allow cleartext only for local `10.0.2.2` (Android emulator) / `localhost` (iOS simulator). (2) **Auth lifecycle** — the exact six-call sequence (register -> OTP verify -> authenticated calls -> refresh -> logout) with a request/response example for each, the OTP resend and attempt-limit rules from task 52, the access-token lifetime, and the **rotation + reuse-detection** contract: the server revokes the presented refresh token on every use, so a client that retries a refresh with the same token gets a 401 and must treat the session as unrecoverable, clear storage, and route to login. Show the Dio interceptor code that implements this and point at `packages/sehatly_api_client`'s tested implementation. (3) **Token storage** — the `flutter_secure_storage` vs `GetStorage` split with the OWASP rationale, plus the `android:allowBackup="false"` requirement and the `InvalidKeyException: Failed to unwrap key` failure it prevents. (4) **Error catalogue** — a table of every status the API returns (200/201/204, 401, 403 with the three distinct 403 causes including the PDP-consent one, 404, 409, 422, 429 with `Retry-After`, 500), the exact error envelope, and a decision tree mapping each to the right client behaviour. (5) **Pagination** — `?page=&per_page=`, the `meta` block, the 100 cap, and the rule that `konsultasi/{id}/chat` is the one **ascending** list. (6) **Date, time and timezone** — the two-rule policy from task 51: instants are ISO-8601 UTC with a `Z` suffix; `DATE` and `TIME` fields are Asia/Jakarta wall-clock and must be parsed with **no** offset conversion, with a worked example showing why `23:30` must not become `16:30`, and a warning never to use `toIso8601String()` when formatting `tanggal_kunjungan`. (7) **File uploads** — which endpoints accept multipart, the field names, the response shape, and the storage URL handling. (8) **Masked and sensitive fields** — the NIK mask format, that `kata_sandi_hash` never appears, and that OTP codes are returned in the response **only** when the server is in the `local` environment. (9) **Realtime chat** — the Pusher protocol summary, channel naming, the `/api/broadcasting/auth` endpoint and the mandatory `Authorization` header on it, subscribe/unsubscribe, the `chat.pesan` event payload, the reconnect + no-replay rule, and the de-duplication requirement. (10) **Push notifications** — device registration, the two platform setup traps, and token refresh. (11) **Contoh integrasi dengan GetX** — because the mobile team is building with GetX, and re-deriving how to wrap `sehatly_api_client` is exactly the rework this section exists to prevent. It must contain, as runnable-shaped Dart: (a) an `AuthController extends GetxController` that calls **`sehatly_api_client` only** — `await Get.find<SehatlyApiClient>().auth.otpVerify(...)` — and never touches `Dio` or `http` directly, with the reactive state exposed as `.obs` and the async transitions driven by `state(...)` / `RxStatus` so loading, empty and error are handled by one consistent idiom; (b) an `AuthBinding extends Bindings` whose `dependencies()` calls `Get.lazyPut<AuthController>(...)` and `Get.lazyPut<SehatlyApiClient>(...)`, showing where the single `SehatlyApiClient` instance and its `TokenStore` are constructed once; (c) the `GetStorage` vs `flutter_secure_storage` split restated in GetX terms — `GetStorage` for theme, locale, cached profile and the FCM token, `flutter_secure_storage` for the access and refresh tokens, matching the already-decided Q4 split that `packages/sehatly_api_client`'s `TokenStore` interface implements; and (d) a note that the bindings use the **`Bindings` + `void dependencies()` + `Get.lazyPut` API of `get: ^4.7.3`**, because the 5.0.0 release candidate deprecates `Bindings`/`BindingsBuilder` in favour of an unreleased `Binding` + `List<Bind>` API that does not exist in stable — so pinning `get: ^4.7.3` in the consuming app is part of following this example. **This section is documentation only:** it must not create a `mobile/` directory, a `pubspec.yaml` with an `sdk: flutter` constraint, or any `.dart` file outside `packages/sehatly_api_client/` itself. (12) **Server-side rules the client must NOT re-implement** — a table of every rule the server owns, so the Flutter team does not duplicate it and get it subtly wrong: slot availability depends on `dokter_libur` **and** `kuota_per_sesi` **and** active bookings, so never compute it client-side; `resep.berlaku_sampai` is `tanggal_resep + 7 days` and expiry is **computed, never stored** — there is no `kadaluarsa` trigger, so trust the server's `is_kedaluwarsa` flag rather than comparing dates locally; a slot is free only while active bookings `< kuota_per_sesi` (default 1); `is_rujukan`/`is_konsultasi_lanjutan` never produce an automatic discount; a `surat_rujukan` requires prior `berbagi_data_medis` PDP consent and the server returns 403 without it; `resep_verifikasi` allows exactly **one** verification per prescription, so a `ditolak` result is terminal and there is no resubmit path; drug-interaction warnings are advisory — the server returns them in the response and does not block, but a `kontraindikasi` item requires a non-empty `catatan_dokter`; webhook/payment state changes asynchronously, so poll `GET /invoice/{id}` rather than assuming the pay call settled it; and stock is decremented server-side at payment, so the client's displayed total can legitimately differ from the post-payment order state. (13) **Endpoint cookbook** — a copy-pasteable Dart snippet per module using the `SehatlyApiClient` typed endpoints. (14) **Troubleshooting** — a symptom-to-cause table covering 401 loops, 403 on a private channel, empty doctor lists, slots that never refresh, `is_kedaluwarsa` confusion, and interaction warnings that look wrong. Every code block must be copied from a file that actually exists in the repo, and every endpoint listed must appear in `php artisan route:list --path=api/v1`. Must NOT invent endpoints, must NOT describe behaviour the code does not have, must NOT duplicate the generated OpenAPI spec by hand.

- [x] 38. Implement `ObatInteraksiService`: bidirectional interactions, cross-prescription history, and allergy matching
  What to do / Must NOT do: **TDD this one.** Create `app/Services/Obat/ObatInteraksiService.php` with three methods, all returning structured arrays and **never throwing**, so the doctor decides. (1) `cekAntarItem(array $resepIds)` — for every ordered pair of `resep_item` rows in the same prescription, look up `obat_interaksi` in **both directions**, because `UNIQUE KEY uq_interaksi (obat_a_id, obat_b_id)` (`:738`) stores one direction only and a single-direction query silently misses half of all interactions. Emit `{tingkat, deskripsi, obat_a, obat_b}`; `berat` and `kontraindikasi` are the warning threshold the spec names. (2) `cekRiwayatPasien(int $pasienId, int $obatId)` — the spec's second requirement: join `resep_item` across the patient's **other** prescriptions with `resep.status IN ('aktif','diproses')` and check each against the new drug, so a clash with a still-active prior prescription surfaces, not just a clash inside one prescription. (3) `cekAlergi(int $pasienId, array $obatIds)` — match `pasien_alergi` rows with `tipe_alergen = 'obat'` against the candidates. **This matching has no reliable join key, and the method docblock must say so:** `pasien_alergi.nama_alergen` is free text (`:277`), **not** a foreign key to `master_obat`; `resep_item.obat_id` is nullable for racikan (`:770`); `resep_item.nama_obat` is a name *snapshot* (`:771`). So normalise both sides (lowercase, strip non-alphanumerics, collapse whitespace), match the snapshot text first, and fall back to `kelas_terapi` matching for `ringan`/`sedang` severity only. Skip NULL `obat_id` racikan items entirely and say so. Treat `pasien.catatan_alergi` (`:242`) as read-only free text — it is a second, unsynchronised source. Every warning carries a `sumber` field (`antar_item` / `riwayat_resep` / `alergi`) so the UI can group them. Because there is no `resep_interaksi` table and no acknowledgement column, the doctor's decision to proceed despite a `kontraindikasi` can only be recorded as free text in `resep.catatan_dokter` — so make the API **require** a non-empty `catatan_dokter` when a `kontraindikasi` item is added, returning 422 otherwise. Record all of this in `docs/schema-notes.md`. Must NOT block a prescription automatically, must NOT query only one direction, must NOT claim racikan are interaction-checked.

- [ ] 43. Add global audit-log observers for every sensitive model
  What to do / Must NOT do: Create `app/Observers/LogsAudit.php` and register it **globally** — via the `#[ObservedBy(AuditObserver::class)]` attribute on each audited model, or a single registration in `AppServiceProvider` — for `RekamMedis`, `RekamMedisDiagnosa`, `RekamMedisTindakan`, `RekamMedisLampiran`, `RekamMedisPersetujuan`, `Resep`, `ResepItem`, `ResepVerifikasi`, `Pasien`, `PasienAlergi`, `PasienAnggotaKeluarga`, `Booking`, `Konsultasi`, `KonsultasiChat`, `SuratKeterangan`, `Rujukan`, `AksesRekamMedisLog`'s parent chain, `Invoice`, `Pembayaran`, `PromoRedemption`, and `User`. The spec is explicit: use an observer or trait, **never** manual logging in controllers. The observer maps Eloquent events to `audit_log.aksi`: `created`->`create`, `updated`->`update`, `deleted`->`delete`, plus explicit `login`/`logout` writes from the auth service, and `read` for reads routed through `RekamMedisService`. It captures `user_id` from the current auth context, `ip_address`, `user_agent`, and `endpoint` from the request, and a `tabel_target`/`record_id` pair (`record_id` is `VARCHAR(64)`, so a string cast is required). **Do not snapshot whole models:** `data_lama`/`data_baru` are JSON columns and a full snapshot of a `rekam_medis` row would bloat the table — use a per-model **column allow-list** and redact `pasien.nik` and `users.kata_sandi_hash` to a hash prefix. `audit_log` is append-only with only `dibuat_at`, so the model needs `$timestamps = false`. **Compliance note to record in `docs/schema-notes.md`:** `akses_rekam_medis_log.rekam_medis_id` has `ON DELETE CASCADE` (`:1153`), so hard-deleting a medical record destroys its own access evidence — therefore medical records must only ever be soft-deleted, and no code path may call `forceDelete()` on `RekamMedis` or its children. Add an architecture test that fails if any **controller or service** contains a direct `AuditLog::create(` call, proving the observer really is the only writer — and note in a comment that login/logout are *not* Eloquent model events, so `TokenService` writes those two rows explicitly by design and the grep is scoped to `app/Http/Controllers/` to avoid a contradiction with that intent (Oracle finding: the earlier "observers only, never manual" rule is only half-enforceable because two `aksi` values are not model events). Must NOT log inside controllers, must NOT snapshot unredacted sensitive columns, must NOT allow hard deletion of `rekam_medis`.
  Parallelization: Wave 8 | Blocked by: 4, 19, 33 | Blocks: 44, 47, 53 | Can parallelize with: 44
  References (executor has NO interview context - be exhaustive): `telemedicine_test.sql:1118-1132` (`audit_log`), `:1147-1155` (`akses_rekam_medis_log` + the CASCADE at `:1153`); spec §2 Modul 5 rule "wajib masuk `audit_log` (middleware/observer Eloquent, jangan manual di tiap controller)"; draft F8
  Acceptance criteria (agent-executable): a test creates a `RekamMedis` and asserts exactly one `audit_log` row with `aksi = 'create'`; a test updates it and asserts one `aksi = 'update'` row whose `data_lama`/`data_baru` contain **only** allow-listed columns; a test asserts `pasien.nik` never appears verbatim in any `data_lama`/`data_baru`; a test asserts logging in and out produces `aksi = 'login'` and `'logout'`; **a test asserts no PHI column is duplicated into `audit_log`** — specifically that `RekamMedis`' SOAP text, `Booking.keluhan`, and `KonsultasiChat.isi` are **absent** from `data_lama`/`data_baru` (Oracle finding: an allow-list that includes them would write a second, less-protected copy of protected health information into an append-only table readable under the broader `audit.lihat` permission, widening the blast radius; the allow-list must be deny-by-default per model); **a test asserts `pasien.nik` and `pasien.nik_cipher` are redacted to the masked form rather than hashed** (a hash in an append-only log is a permanent linkable NIK identifier and defeats the PDP purpose); the architecture test proving no controller writes `AuditLog::create(` passes; a test asserts `forceDelete()` on `RekamMedis` is blocked (throw) and that the `akses_rekam_medis_log` rows survive.
  QA scenarios (name the exact tool + invocation): happy - `php artisan test --filter=AuditLoggingTest` covering create/update/delete/login/logout, column allow-listing, and redaction, Evidence `.omo/evidence/task-43-sehatly.md`. failure - (a) write an `AuditLog::create(` call into a controller and assert the architecture test fails, (b) assert `forceDelete()` throws and the access log survives, (c) assert the NIK redaction test fails if redaction is removed, Evidence `.omo/evidence/task-43-sehatly.md`.

- [ ] 44. Implement `InvoiceService` (polymorphic) and promo validation
  What to do / Must NOT do: Create `app/Services/Invoice/InvoiceService.php` with a single generic entry point `buat(string $referensiTipe, int $referensiId, int $pasienId, array $lines, ?int $metodeId = null): Invoice` used by every caller (booking from task 27, consultation, prescription, medicine order from task 46), computing `subtotal`, `diskon`, `biaya_admin` (from `master_metode_pembayaran.biaya_admin_flat` + `biaya_admin_persen` on `subtotal - diskon`), `biaya_pengiriman`, and `total`, then generating `nomor_invoice` (`INV` + `Ymd` + sequence). It must validate ownership by loading the referenced record and confirming its `pasien_id` matches — the polymorphic `referensi_id` has **no** foreign key (`:941`), so referential integrity is entirely the service's job. Only 4 of the 6 `referensi_tipe` values are reachable in Modules 1-5; the other two (`lab_permintaan`, `home_care`) must be explicitly rejected with a clear "not implemented in this scope" 422 rather than an obscure SQL error. **Duplicate-invoice risk:** there is no uniqueness on `(referensi_tipe, referensi_id)`, so `buat()` must guard with an existence check inside a transaction, and `INDEX idx_ref` (`:954`) makes that lookup fast. `POST /api/v1/promo/validasi` takes `{kode, invoice_id}` and returns a **pure calculation** — valid flag, message, computed `nilai_diskon`, and the resulting `total` — with **no writes**, because `promo_redemption.invoice_id` is `NOT NULL` (`:1005`) and a validation call has no invoice to attach to; the redemption row is written later, by `InvoiceService`, when the promo is actually applied. Validation must enforce: `status_aktif = 1`, `mulai_at <= now <= selesai_at`, `subtotal >= min_transaksi`, `maks_diskon` capping for `tipe_diskon = 'persen'`, `kuota_total` remaining, and `kuota_per_user` — the last two are **not** DB-enforced (no unique on `(promo_id, pasien_id)`), so `lockForUpdate()` the `master_promo` row (which always exists) before counting `promo_redemption` rows, inside the same transaction that writes the redemption; a bare count inside a transaction does **not** prevent oversubscription, because two concurrent applies both observe remaining = 1 and both redeem (Oracle finding — this is a real financial-loss race, and the lock costs nothing because the row is guaranteed present). Record the residual race for `kuota_total` under high concurrency in `docs/schema-notes.md`. Handle `tipe_diskon = 'gratis_ongkir'` by zeroing `biaya_pengiriman` rather than by inflating `diskon`. Create `App\Http\Requests\ValidasiPromoRequest` and `App\Http\Resources\{InvoiceResource, PromoResource}`. Must NOT write during validation, must NOT exceed `kuota_per_user`, must NOT allow an invoice for another patient's reference, must NOT support the two out-of-scope reference types.

  Acceptance criteria (agent-executable): `php artisan route:list --path=api/v1/promo` lists 1 route; a test asserts `POST /promo/validasi` creates **zero** `promo_redemption` rows; a test asserts `persen` respects `maks_diskon`; a test asserts `gratis_ongkir` zeroes `biaya_pengiriman` rather than inflating `diskon`; a test asserts a second call to `buat()` for the same `(referensi_tipe, referensi_id)` does not create a duplicate invoice; a test asserts `referensi_tipe = 'lab_permintaan'` returns 422 with a clear message; a test asserts `biaya_admin_persen` is applied to `subtotal - diskon` and not to `subtotal`.
